  "sourceWitnesses": [
    {
      "path": "packages/operator-core/lib/work-items.ts",
      "sha256": "75d40cfbd5d8eb2cd19e2707ee28999dde1ff1d786fc912515b75d2a7e178967",
      "excerptStartLine": 6761,
      "excerptEndLine": 6805,
      "excerpt": " * The watchdog AUTO-CLOSE recovery-window floor for ISSUE-family self-selection\n * (EI-20106946822538304). A watchdog-sourced item is not useful work while the\n * auto-close sweep is still gathering enough evidence to decide whether its signal\n * recovered. This fragment is an INCLUDE/PASS predicate: it excludes only an\n * eligible `<source>:<key>` candidate whose exact newest six ran ticks all have\n * non-NULL `known_open_keys` and none contain that key.\n *\n * Every malformed, keyless, ineligible, short, NULL-bearing, or key-present history\n * fails OPEN. Empty arrays are valid absence evidence. The candidate's workspace is\n * used for the tick history so one workspace cannot suppress another. Keep this in\n * the cycle-safe work-items leaf: importing auto-close.ts here would cycle through\n * issues-engineer.ts. The source allowlist and six-tick window are shared from the\n * leaf constants module instead.\n */\nexport function watchdogRecoveryWindowExclusionSql(sql: OrgSql) {\n  const eligibleSources = [...AUTO_CLOSE_ELIGIBLE_SOURCES];\n  const minTicks = AUTO_CLOSE_DEFAULT_MIN_TICKS;\n  const watchdogKey = `COALESCE(wi.payload, '{}'::jsonb) ->> 'watchdogKey'`;\n  return sql`(\n    ${sql.unsafe(watchdogKey)} IS NULL\n    OR btrim(${sql.unsafe(watchdogKey)}) = ''\n    OR position(':' IN ${sql.unsafe(watchdogKey)}) <= 1\n    OR btrim(substring(\n         ${sql.unsafe(watchdogKey)}\n         FROM position(':' IN ${sql.unsafe(watchdogKey)}) + 1\n       )) = ''\n    OR split_part(${sql.unsafe(watchdogKey)}, ':', 1) <> ALL(${eligibleSources}::text[])\n    OR NOT (\n      SELECT count(*) = ${minTicks}\n         AND count(recovery_ticks.known_open_keys) = ${minTicks}\n         AND count(*) FILTER (\n               WHERE ${sql.unsafe(watchdogKey)} = ANY(recovery_ticks.known_open_keys)\n             ) = 0\n        FROM (\n          SELECT wt.known_open_keys\n            FROM harness_shared.watchdog_ticks wt\n           WHERE wt.workspace_id = wi.workspace_id\n             AND wt.status = 'ran'\n           ORDER BY wt.tick_at DESC, wt.id DESC\n           LIMIT ${minTicks}\n        ) recovery_ticks\n    )\n  )`;\n}\n\n",
      "identityLimit": "Current staging tree witness; no historical or loaded runtime identity asserted."
    },
    {
      "path": "packages/operator-core/lib/work-items.ts",
      "sha256": "75d40cfbd5d8eb2cd19e2707ee28999dde1ff1d786fc912515b75d2a7e178967",
      "excerptStartLine": 9313,
      "excerptEndLine": 9315,
      "excerpt": "  'watchdog-recovery-window':\n    'eligible watchdog signal is still inside the auto-close recovery evidence window; wait for six complete ran ticks',\n  'cross-machine-rig': 'needs a ≥2-machine rig this caller does not have (WI-2796)',\n",
      "identityLimit": "Current staging tree witness; no historical or loaded runtime identity asserted."
    },
    {
      "path": "packages/operator-core/lib/harness/improvements/auto-close-sources.ts",
      "sha256": "f636e289f435a42d7d80d6845a1d75af9d77a4a9b78943ac01bfe6a1849b003f",
      "excerptStartLine": 1,
      "excerptEndLine": 163,
      "excerpt": "/**\n * The auto-close-eligible watchdog SOURCE allowlist, in a LEAF module.\n *\n * WHY THIS IS NOT IN `auto-close.ts` (EI-20106946822538304): two subsystems need this set —\n * the auto-close sweep (which CLOSES a recovered item) and the issue-family self-select claim\n * floor (which must not OFFER one during its recovery window). Importing `auto-close.ts` from\n * `work-items.ts` is a cycle: `work-items.ts -> auto-close.ts -> issues-engineer.ts ->\n * work-items.ts`. This module imports NOTHING, so both sides can share the one definition\n * instead of forking a second copy that silently drifts — a forked allowlist would mean the\n * claim floor and the auto-close sweep disagreeing about which sources self-heal, which is\n * exactly the class of bug the shared-oracle discipline elsewhere in the claim path exists to\n * prevent (see `observationLaneExclusionSql`'s \"ONE source of truth shared by the claim and\n * the miss diagnosis\" note).\n *\n * `auto-close.ts` re-exports this symbol, so its public API is unchanged.\n */\n\n/**\n * Sources where a signal's ABSENCE reliably means the problem resolved (the\n * collector runs every tick and would re-fire if it persisted). Conservative\n * allowlist — live-state/one-shot/ambiguous sources stay out so a quiet tick is\n * never mistaken for a resolution.\n */\nexport const AUTO_CLOSE_ELIGIBLE_SOURCES: ReadonlySet<string> = new Set([\n  // ⛔ 'red-test' is DELIBERATELY ABSENT — do not re-add it (template-supply-chain-repair\n  // 2026-08-10 P-003). It is the one source whose input is a LOG OF EXTERNALLY-TRIGGERED\n  // EVENTS (`harness_shared.test_runs`) rather than a live re-derivation of the condition.\n  // Every other entry here re-evaluates its own predicate each tick (a DB query, a claim\n  // table, a routine's `active` flag), so a quiet tick genuinely means \"the condition is\n  // gone\". For red-test a quiet tick means only \"nobody RAN this test\" — the collector\n  // cannot distinguish a test that was fixed from one that was dropped from its suite,\n  // renamed, left un-owned by any vitest config, or whose checkout disappeared.\n  //\n  // Measured case (EI-11141, templates/papercusp-app/checks/composition-integrity\n  // .test.ts): last run 2026-07-27, `resolved` 2026-08-02, and ZERO 'pass' rows in ALL of\n  // test_runs history — the named import (@papercusp/template-kit) was still unresolvable\n  // when the item was closed, and stayed broken for another 8 days. Its runs stopped\n  // because NO vitest config owns templates/**/checks/*.test.ts (testing:run answers\n  // TEST_FILE_ROUTE_ERROR), not because anything was fixed.\n  //\n  // Removing it costs nothing: red-test is also the ONLY source with a POSITIVE-evidence\n  // resolution path (`resolveProvenGreenRedTests`, P-007), which resolves a genuinely-fixed\n  // test on N consecutive recorded greens and attaches them as real completion evidence.\n  // Absence-closing it was strictly the weaker, unfalsifiable half of that pair.\n  // ⛔ 'repeated-tool-error' is DELIBERATELY ABSENT. A quiet error-observation window\n  // only proves that the failing call was not observed; it does not prove that the\n  // underlying deployed tool schema or handler changed. In particular, traffic can\n  // stop after a schema rejection, making absence indistinguishable from an idle\n  // monitor. Require positive verification (or an explicit fix/deploy reference)\n  // before retiring this source; absence-only auto-close is unsafe here.\n  'stalled-feature',\n  'unresolved-escalation',\n  'failed-spawn',\n  'smoke-fail',\n  'escalation-spike',\n  'fire-circuit-open',\n  // P-011: per-harness sources whose absence reliably means resolution (a torn-down\n  // benchmark hive's saturation/orphan/stuck signal simply stops). Added so auto-close\n  // retires the full benchmark-debris set (governor-starvation was the gap — 27 EIs).\n  'governor-starvation',\n  // P-009 (producer-silence.ts): re-derived from PG every tick, latches nothing — a quiet\n  // tick means the producer produced again, or was switched off on purpose (both genuine\n  // resolutions). A failed output read makes the collector THROW (learning-slo.ts\n  // producerSilenceCollector), so an unmeasured tick is never counted as an absent one.\n  'producer-silence',\n  'orphaned-spawn',\n  'stuck-plan',\n  'ship-link-stuck',\n  // agent-activity-liveness-truth P-007: a live-state collector — when the claim\n  // progresses (or is freed/reclaimed) the stalled signal simply stops, so its\n  // absence reliably means resolution.\n  'stalled-claim',\n  // WI-38327: collectBridgeNeedsOwnerSignals re-derives its whole predicate from PG every\n  // tick (the escalation row's needs_owner flag JOINed against the attention_notifications\n  // audit) and caches/latches nothing, so a quiet tick means one of the two genuine\n  // resolutions: the block cleared, or the owner rail now holds a record of it. The second\n  // is permanent — the EXISTS is cause-matched with no time window, so once the alarm\n  // lands it keeps matching for the life of that episode rather than ageing back into a\n  // violation. A THROWING tick is not an absent one: the sweep counts ran-ticks only.\n  'bridge-needs-owner-unalerted',\n  // EI-5994: collectLoopStalledSignals filters `r.active = true` — once the dead-owner\n  // reaper (loop-unreachable-guard / autoPauseLoopRoutine) deactivates the routine, this\n  // signal STOPS firing immediately and unconditionally (not just recency-gated), so its\n  // absence reliably means the underlying loop is gone/paused, not merely quiet.\n  'loop-stalled',\n  // WI-6554700010029793: collectRoutineStarvationSignals re-derives its predicate from PG every\n  // tick and latches nothing. The signal stops when the routine fires again (next_fire_at\n  // advances past the bound), is deactivated, or parks. It also stops if the WHOLE engine\n  // dies (its EXISTS term needs another routine firing); routine-engine-death raises that case\n  // on its own key. Its registration declares source, so a thrown tick is never a quiet one.\n  'routine-starvation',\n  // EI-1760: collectMigrationDriftSignals re-derives `missing` from a live DB query\n  // EVERY tick (never caches/latches) and, unlike service-down's freshness-gated probe\n  // skip, degrades LOUD not silent on failure — checkMigrationDrift's own internal\n  // catch treats an unreachable schema_migrations tracker as \"report all on-disk as\n  // missing\", so a query outage still FIRES the signal rather than going quiet. A quiet\n  // tick therefore reliably means every on-disk migration is applied (the common case:\n  // the operator restarted and boot-apply caught up) — the exact condition this EI was\n  // filed to auto-resolve instead of leaving self-cleared criticals polluting the queue.\n  'migration-drift',\n  // EI-18143028205990222 class: perf-regression-rig.ts's `consider()` re-derives EVERY\n  // metric (loop-lag-p95, conn-saturation, dispatch-orphan-rate, coord-open-escalations,\n  // adv-tab-switch-median, adv-fcp) from a LIVE query/read each tick — none of them cache\n  // or latch a stale verdict — so a quiet tick reliably means the metric has dropped back\n  // under budget, the same reasoning as `migration-drift` above. Without this, a SLO\n  // breach whose root cause is already fixed elsewhere (e.g. dispatch-orphan-rate reading\n  // a trailing 24h window full of PRE-FIX orphan rows — verified zero new orphans for 4h+\n  // after durable-spawn.ts's EI-18118495431535177 fix landed) has NO path back to closed:\n  // the collector keeps re-deriving the same true-but-stale elevated number every tick\n  // until the window ages the old rows out (hours), and nothing ever retires the EI in\n  // the meantime — every future transient perf-regression breach would hit the same dead\n  // end. dispatchOrphanRate/connSaturationPct/etc. dropping below budget on a later tick\n  // is exactly the \"signal absent from known-open keys\" condition this sweep exists for.\n  'perf-regression',\n  // EI-19417016857494865: collectRoutineFailureSignals re-derives from a LIVE query every\n  // tick (`active = true AND metadata->>'last_error' <> ''`) and never caches or latches —\n  // the same reasoning as `loop-stalled` and `migration-drift` above. The routine RUNNER\n  // deletes `last_error`/`last_error_at`/`last_error_source` on its success path\n  // (routines-workflow.ts, source-scoped to 'runner'), so the signal stops the moment a\n  // routine fires cleanly again; deactivation also stops it unconditionally via the\n  // `active = true` filter. A quiet tick therefore reliably means the routine recovered.\n  // Without this, an instantaneous self-clearing failure (e.g. git-sync hitting one\n  // transient GitHub `Connection reset by peer` on a ~2min cadence) is escalated to a\n  // PERSISTENT claimable MAJOR bug that nothing ever retires: measured 2026-08-03, the\n  // `routine:git-sync` key alone re-filed 3× in 3 days and 12 such EIs since 07-11 were\n  // each hand-drained by a separate agent burning a full claim cycle on an\n  // already-recovered routine. The open-dup guard only suppresses a re-file WHILE one is\n  // open, so draining one merely clears the way for the next.\n  'routine-failure',\n  // WI-40769: ADMITTED, and the two things that previously made it unsafe are now\n  // both closed. Before this, service-down could be RAISED but never LOWERED —\n  // measured 2026-08-23: `service-down:operator` (EI-20100362973577553) open 12\n  // days asserting nothing is listening while the operator answered 200 in 86ms,\n  // and `service-down:staging-api` (EI-19478400954666484) open 19 days. Three of\n  // the five non-terminal service-down rows all-time were stale in exactly this\n  // way. Note the defect was never OVER-FILING — `payload.watchdogKey` dedup works\n  // (one open row per service, not a flood); it was that a filed row had no path\n  // back to closed, so a self-clearing outage became permanent claimable backlog.\n  //\n  // 1. THE COLLECTOR NO LONGER GOES BLIND SILENTLY. `serviceDownCollectorResult`\n  //    used to skip collection on a stale service-health snapshot (the P-004\n  //    freshness gate), which is what the `migration-drift` entry above cites as\n  //    the disqualifier. It now runs a fresh bounded `probeAll()` INLINE on a\n  //    stale snapshot and emits off that, and if the inline probe itself throws it\n  //    emits a LOUD `service-down:monitor-degraded` signal. So it degrades loud,\n  //    not silent — the same property that qualified migration-drift. Measured\n  //    over 3 days: 288 ran-ticks, 286 clean, 2 on the inline-probe fallback, 0 on\n  //    the legacy skip.\n  // 2. THE REMAINING BLIND PATHS ARE NOW DECLARED, NOT INFERRED. The legacy skip\n  //    (PAPERCUSP_WATCHDOG_SERVICE_HEALTH_INLINE_PROBE=0) and the inline-probe\n  //    failure both return `observed:false, unobservedSource:'service-down'`, and\n  //    `decideAutoClose` subtracts those ticks from the absence evidence. So a\n  //    monitor outage can no longer read as \"every service recovered\" — the sweep\n  //    waits for ticks where the collector actually looked.\n  //\n  // ⚠ Do NOT re-add a source here on the strength of (1) alone. It is (2) that\n  // makes a can-go-blind collector safe: without a declared unobserved tick, a\n  // quiet tick and a blind tick are INDISTINGUISHABLE in the tick record.\n  'service-down',\n]);\n\n/** The same evidence window used by the auto-close sweep and its claim floor. */\nexport const AUTO_CLOSE_DEFAULT_MIN_TICKS = 6;\n",
      "identityLimit": "Current staging tree witness; no historical or loaded runtime identity asserted."
    },
    {
      "path": "packages/operator-core/lib/harness/improvements/auto-close.ts",
      "sha256": "9b68c9730108074b4e3a48df9534621f7ffab1572861c7c580d7c6ed41213fcf",
      "excerptStartLine": 92,
      "excerptEndLine": 145,
      "excerpt": "export interface AutoCloseCandidate {\n  issueId: string;\n  /** payload.watchdogKey (`<source>:<key>`). */\n  watchdogKey: string;\n  /** Stable lifecycle identity persisted under payload.toolFailureProbation. */\n  classKey?: string;\n  /** Contract identity persisted under payload.toolFailureProbation. */\n  contractFingerprint?: string;\n  /** Serving revision persisted under payload.toolFailureProbation. */\n  deployedRevision?: string;\n  state: string;\n  assignee: string | null;\n  /** ms epoch of created_at. */\n  createdAtMs: number;\n  /** provenance (organic | drill | replay | shadow). */\n  signalOrigin: string;\n  /** An open, unresolved dispatch ledger row exists for this item. */\n  inFlightDispatch: boolean;\n}\n\n/** The recent-tick context the decision reads against. */\nexport interface AutoCloseTickContext {\n  /** Union of `known_open_keys` across the last `ranTickCount` ran-ticks (the\n   *  \"still-firing\" set). A key absent from this set was absent from ALL of them. */\n  seenKeys: ReadonlySet<string>;\n  /** How many ran-ticks the window held (must be ≥ minAbsentTicks to act). */\n  ranTickCount: number;\n  /**\n   * (WI-40769) Per-source count of ticks IN THIS WINDOW where the source's\n   * collector declared itself UNOBSERVED (`CollectorStatus.observed === false`):\n   * it skipped, or it threw. Those ticks are not absence evidence — the\n   * condition was never evaluated — so `decideAutoClose` subtracts them.\n   *\n   * This is what makes a source whose collector CAN go blind safe to\n   * absence-close: instead of excluding the source forever, the sweep simply\n   * waits until enough ticks exist where the collector actually looked. Absent\n   * / empty map ⇒ every tick observed (the pre-WI-40769 assumption, which is\n   * correct for every collector that never declares itself blind).\n   */\n  unobservedTicksBySource?: ReadonlyMap<string, number>;\n  nowMs: number;\n}\n\nexport interface AutoCloseOptions {\n  /** Consecutive ran-ticks a signal must be absent before its EI auto-closes. Default 6 (~90min). */\n  minAbsentTicks?: number;\n  /** An EI younger than this never auto-closes (its signal may not have hit known-open yet). Default 90min. */\n  minIssueAgeMs?: number;\n  /**\n   * P-007: consecutive proven-green runs required to auto-resolve a `red-test` EI on\n   * POSITIVE evidence (before the absence path considers it). Default\n   * RED_TEST_GREEN_MIN_CONSECUTIVE (3). See red-test-green-resolve.ts.\n   */\n  redTestGreenMinConsecutive?: number;\n",
      "identityLimit": "Current staging tree witness; no historical or loaded runtime identity asserted."
    },
    {
      "path": "packages/operator-core/lib/harness/improvements/auto-close.ts",
      "sha256": "9b68c9730108074b4e3a48df9534621f7ffab1572861c7c580d7c6ed41213fcf",
      "excerptStartLine": 151,
      "excerptEndLine": 201,
      "excerpt": "\nexport interface AutoCloseDecision {\n  close: boolean;\n  reason: string;\n}\n\n/**\n * Pure: should this candidate auto-close? Returns `{close:false, reason}` for every\n * guard so the decision is fully observable + unit-tested. Closes ONLY when the\n * signal has verifiably stopped firing and the item is safe to retire untouched.\n */\nexport function decideAutoClose(\n  c: AutoCloseCandidate,\n  ctx: AutoCloseTickContext,\n  opts: AutoCloseOptions = {},\n): AutoCloseDecision {\n  const minTicks = opts.minAbsentTicks ?? AUTO_CLOSE_DEFAULT_MIN_TICKS;\n  const minAge = opts.minIssueAgeMs ?? AUTO_CLOSE_DEFAULT_MIN_AGE_MS;\n  if (c.state !== 'open') return { close: false, reason: 'not-open' };\n  if (!c.watchdogKey) return { close: false, reason: 'no-watchdog-key' };\n  if (effectiveOrigin(c.signalOrigin) !== DEFAULT_SIGNAL_ORIGIN) return { close: false, reason: 'non-organic' };\n  if (!AUTO_CLOSE_ELIGIBLE_SOURCES.has(watchdogSourceOf(c.watchdogKey))) {\n    return { close: false, reason: 'ineligible-source' };\n  }\n  // EI-7317: an `assignee` alone must NOT block retirement — only a LIVE in-flight\n  // dispatch means genuinely active work. Once a dispatch resolves (or a watchdog\n  // routine it was chasing is deactivated/deleted) the `assignee` column is never\n  // cleared, so a signal that has verifiably stopped firing was staying open+assigned\n  // forever and getting re-dispatched indefinitely (concrete case: EI-3901 — the\n  // underlying loop was deactivated 2026-07-01, but the EI re-dispatched on\n  // 2026-07-04, burning a full worker run to rediscover the bug was already gone).\n  // inFlightDispatch is the only signal that means \"do not touch, someone is on it\".\n  if (c.inFlightDispatch) return { close: false, reason: 'in-flight-dispatch' };\n  if (ctx.ranTickCount < minTicks) return { close: false, reason: 'insufficient-tick-history' };\n  // WI-40769: discount ticks where THIS source's collector observed nothing (it\n  // skipped, or it threw). A ran-tick proves the WATCHDOG ran, not that this\n  // particular collector evaluated its predicate — and silence from a collector\n  // that never looked is not evidence that the condition cleared. Counted per\n  // source so one blind collector cannot excuse another's absence.\n  const unobserved = ctx.unobservedTicksBySource?.get(watchdogSourceOf(c.watchdogKey)) ?? 0;\n  if (ctx.ranTickCount - unobserved < minTicks) {\n    return { close: false, reason: 'insufficient-observed-tick-history' };\n  }\n  if (!Number.isFinite(c.createdAtMs) || ctx.nowMs - c.createdAtMs < minAge) {\n    return { close: false, reason: 'too-new' };\n  }\n  if (ctx.seenKeys.has(c.watchdogKey)) return { close: false, reason: 'still-firing' };\n  const abandonedAssignmentNote = c.assignee != null ? ' (assignee present but no live in-flight dispatch — abandoned assignment, not active work)' : '';\n  return {\n    close: true,\n    reason: `signal absent from known-open keys for ≥${minTicks} ran-ticks — evidence cleared${abandonedAssignmentNote}`,\n",
      "identityLimit": "Current staging tree witness; no historical or loaded runtime identity asserted."
    },
    {
      "path": "packages/operator-core/lib/harness/improvements/auto-close.ts",
      "sha256": "9b68c9730108074b4e3a48df9534621f7ffab1572861c7c580d7c6ed41213fcf",
      "excerptStartLine": 311,
      "excerptEndLine": 342,
      "excerpt": "      }\n      return rows;\n    },\n    recentRanTickKeys: async (n: number) => {\n      // WI-40769: `collectors` comes back alongside the keys so a BLIND tick is\n      // distinguishable from a QUIET one. It is the existing jsonb column — no\n      // migration — and pre-WI-40769 rows simply carry no `observed` field,\n      // which reads as observed:true and preserves the old behaviour exactly.\n      const rows = await sql<{ known_open_keys: string[]; collectors: unknown }[]>`\n        SELECT known_open_keys, collectors\n          FROM harness_shared.watchdog_ticks\n         WHERE workspace_id = ${workspaceId} AND status = 'ran'\n         ORDER BY tick_at DESC\n         LIMIT ${n}`;\n      const seenKeys = new Set<string>();\n      const unobservedTicksBySource = new Map<string, number>();\n      for (const r of rows) {\n        for (const k of r.known_open_keys ?? []) seenKeys.add(k);\n        // Count each source at most ONCE per tick: a source with several\n        // collectors must not subtract more than the one tick it lost.\n        const blindHere = new Set<string>();\n        for (const c of Array.isArray(r.collectors) ? r.collectors : []) {\n          if (!c || typeof c !== 'object') continue;\n          const st = c as { observed?: unknown; unobservedSource?: unknown };\n          if (st.observed !== false) continue;\n          if (typeof st.unobservedSource !== 'string' || !st.unobservedSource) continue;\n          blindHere.add(st.unobservedSource);\n        }\n        for (const s of blindHere) unobservedTicksBySource.set(s, (unobservedTicksBySource.get(s) ?? 0) + 1);\n      }\n      return { seenKeys, ranTickCount: rows.length, unobservedTicksBySource };\n    },\n",
      "identityLimit": "Current staging tree witness; no historical or loaded runtime identity asserted."
    },
    {
      "path": "packages/operator-core/lib/harness/improvements/auto-close.ts",
      "sha256": "9b68c9730108074b4e3a48df9534621f7ffab1572861c7c580d7c6ed41213fcf",
      "excerptStartLine": 350,
      "excerptEndLine": 366,
      "excerpt": "    },\n    close: async (id, watchdogKey, reason) => {\n      await commentIssue(\n        id,\n        `🟢 Auto-resolved by the watchdog: ${reason}. Signal \\`${watchdogKey}\\` stopped firing — no fix was dispatched; the underlying condition cleared on its own. If the exact signal recurs, the recurrence REOPENS this row (P-004 exact-key recurrence reopen) rather than filing a sibling. (P-009)`,\n        WATCHDOG_AUTO_CLOSE_OWNER,\n      );\n      // skipCompletionGate (WI-1403/WI-1404, contract C-1): the watchdog auto-close is a\n      // DEDUP marker, not a genuine completion — \"no fix was dispatched; the underlying\n      // condition cleared on its own\" is explicitly NOT evidence of completed work, so\n      // this flip must never satisfy C-1's genuine-completion criteria.\n      // P-004: the `by` identity below lands in terminalOwner — capture-core's\n      // exact-key recurrence reopen matches on exactly this value, so the promise\n      // in the comment above is mechanically kept.\n      await setIssueState(id, 'resolved', WATCHDOG_AUTO_CLOSE_OWNER, undefined, { skipCompletionGate: true });\n    },\n    // P-007: recent test_runs for red-test paths, LATEST-FIRST, keyed by the SAME\n",
      "identityLimit": "Current staging tree witness; no historical or loaded runtime identity asserted."
    },
    {
      "path": "packages/operator-core/lib/harness/improvements/auto-close.ts",
      "sha256": "9b68c9730108074b4e3a48df9534621f7ffab1572861c7c580d7c6ed41213fcf",
      "excerptStartLine": 439,
      "excerptEndLine": 510,
      "excerpt": " * owner enables `papercusp-watchdog-auto-close`. Reads open watchdog-keyed EIs, the\n * recent ran-tick known-open keys, and the in-flight dispatch set; closes each EI the\n * pure decision admits.\n */\nexport async function processAutoClose(\n  workspaceId: string,\n  opts: AutoCloseOptions = {},\n  deps?: AutoCloseDeps,\n): Promise<AutoCloseOutcome[]> {\n  if (!(await getFlag(FLAGS.WATCHDOG_AUTO_CLOSE, `watchdog-auto-close:${workspaceId}`))) return [];\n  const d = deps ?? defaultAutoCloseDeps(workspaceId);\n  const minTicks = opts.minAbsentTicks ?? AUTO_CLOSE_DEFAULT_MIN_TICKS;\n\n  const open = await d.listOpen();\n  // Pre-filter to watchdog-keyed candidates before the (cheaper) tick + dispatch reads.\n  const keyed = open.filter((i) => watchdogKeyOfIssue(i));\n  if (keyed.length === 0) return [];\n  const out: AutoCloseOutcome[] = [];\n\n  const inFlight = await d.inFlightDispatchIds(keyed.map((i) => i.id));\n\n  // ── P-007: POSITIVE green-run resolution ────────────────────────────────────────────\n  // Runs BEFORE (and independent of) the absence path, and does NOT need ran-tick history:\n  // it resolves a red-test EI whose named test verifiably passes NOW (N consecutive greens\n  // in test_runs), attaching that as real completion evidence. A green-resolved item is\n  // then skipped by the absence loop below.\n  const greenResolved = await resolveProvenGreenRedTests(keyed, inFlight, d, opts, out);\n\n  // ── Positive repeated-tool-error repair resolution ──────────────────────────────────\n  // Unlike the absence path, this path needs no quiet-tick history: an exact class/key\n  // contract change or passing probe is affirmative evidence that the rejected call was\n  // repaired. The source remains absent from AUTO_CLOSE_ELIGIBLE_SOURCES, so this is the\n  // only route by which repeated-tool-error can close.\n  const toolFailureResolved = await resolveProvenToolFailureRepairs(keyed, inFlight, d, out);\n\n  // ── Absence-based close (needs enough ran-tick history) ────────────────────────────\n  const { seenKeys, ranTickCount, unobservedTicksBySource } = await d.recentRanTickKeys(minTicks);\n  if (ranTickCount >= minTicks) {\n    const nowMs = d.nowMs();\n    for (const i of keyed) {\n      if (greenResolved.has(i.id) || toolFailureResolved.has(i.id)) continue; // already resolved positively\n      const watchdogKey = watchdogKeyOfIssue(i);\n      const candidate: AutoCloseCandidate = {\n        issueId: i.id,\n        watchdogKey,\n        ...toolFailureRepairFieldsOf(i),\n        state: i.state,\n        assignee: i.assignee,\n        createdAtMs: i.createdAt ? Date.parse(i.createdAt) : NaN,\n        signalOrigin: i.signalOrigin ?? DEFAULT_SIGNAL_ORIGIN,\n        inFlightDispatch: inFlight.has(i.id),\n      };\n      const decision = decideAutoClose(\n        candidate,\n        { seenKeys, ranTickCount, unobservedTicksBySource, nowMs },\n        opts,\n      );\n      if (!decision.close) continue;\n      try {\n        await d.close(i.id, watchdogKey, decision.reason);\n        out.push({ issueId: i.id, watchdogKey, reason: decision.reason });\n      } catch (e) {\n        // Best-effort: one close failing must not abort the sweep.\n        console.warn(`[watchdog-auto-close] close failed for ${i.id}:`, e instanceof Error ? e.message : e);\n      }\n    }\n  }\n  if (out.length > 0) {\n    console.log(`[watchdog-auto-close] retired ${out.length} watchdog EI(s): ${out.map((o) => o.issueId).join(', ')}`);\n  }\n  return out;\n}\n",
      "identityLimit": "Current staging tree witness; no historical or loaded runtime identity asserted."
    }
  ],

          "family": "issue",
          "harness": "papercusp",
          "title": "Service staging-api is WEDGED (listening, not accepting)",
          "summary": "Watchdog signal (service-down): \"staging-api\" is still LISTENING but is not accepting connections — 14 queued unaccepted of a 511 backlog. Its event loop is blocked, so every other liveness signal still reads healthy: the unit is active, the pid is alive, and a TCP connect succeeds (the kernel completes the handshake without the process). A restart RECOVERS it but destroys the evidence — capture logs and RSS FIRST, or the cause survives the restart. Probe note: WEDGED, not down: a process is still listening on this port but has 14 connection(s) queued unaccepted (backlog 511) — its event loop is blocked, so systemd/is-active, a live pid and a TCP connect all still look healthy. A restart clears the symptom only; find what is blocking the loop.",

          "admissible": false,
          "refusedBy": "admission-pending",
          "failedFloors": [
            "admission-pending",
            "admission-hold"
          ],
          "detail": "duplicate screening is pending for this WORK-ITEM CLAIM. It does not block opening an agent or fleet process; launch and queue admission are separate capabilities. A launched agent still needs claimable or validly pre-assigned work. This row carries DUPLICATE CANDIDATES found when it was filed, at least one stronger than a soft semantic match, so only the promoter's model review can admit it (as distinct) or merge it into its twin. The promoter runs on a 30-minute tick but reviews only a few rows per tick, candidate rows first, so this wait has NO fixed bound; the independent fail-open runner deliberately never admits a row with unresolved strong candidates. It still CLEARS ON ITS OWN, and it is not a permanent refusal — but do not wait on it: check whether a named candidate is the same problem and work that row instead, or use a create-time admission route for genuinely distinct work.",
          "failOpenExclusion": "duplicate-candidates",
          "remedy": "Choose the work route before creating a replacement. Existing-plan fleet work should use plans:start then fleet:launch-on-plan; its promoted items use the plan-item bypass. Fixed ad-hoc work should be created atomically with assign_to naming the intended owner, then launched with capability:launch-agent. Any one of these makes a NEW item born admitted: the item implements a plan item; severity:'critical'; a 'security' topic; assign_to names the intended owner. These are create-time admission bypasses, not blanket authority: assign_to is still checked against the target's live fleet scope, dependencies, locks, security, credentials and resource admission; a scope mismatch files the item unassigned and explains why. Launching does not change an ALREADY-pending item. Wait for its promoter/backstop instead of duplicating or re-filing it; only the existing server-derived dispatch/force paths may grant their narrower exceptions."
        },

          "detail": "duplicate screening is pending for this WORK-ITEM CLAIM. It does not block opening an agent or fleet process; launch and queue admission are separate capabilities. A launched agent still needs claimable or validly pre-assigned work. This row carries DUPLICATE CANDIDATES found when it was filed, at least one stronger than a soft semantic match, so only the promoter's model review can admit it (as distinct) or merge it into its twin. The promoter runs on a 30-minute tick but reviews only a few rows per tick, candidate rows first, so this wait has NO fixed bound; the independent fail-open runner deliberately never admits a row with unresolved strong candidates. It still CLEARS ON ITS OWN, and it is not a permanent refusal — but do not wait on it: check whether a named candidate is the same problem and work that row instead, or use a create-time admission route for genuinely distinct work.",
          "failOpenExclusion": "duplicate-candidates",
          "remedy": "Choose the work route before creating a replacement. Existing-plan fleet work should use plans:start then fleet:launch-on-plan; its promoted items use the plan-item bypass. Fixed ad-hoc work should be created atomically with assign_to naming the intended owner, then launched with capability:launch-agent. Any one of these makes a NEW item born admitted: the item implements a plan item; severity:'critical'; a 'security' topic; assign_to names the intended owner. These are create-time admission bypasses, not blanket authority: assign_to is still checked against the target's live fleet scope, dependencies, locks, security, credentials and resource admission; a scope mismatch files the item unassigned and explains why. Launching does not change an ALREADY-pending item. Wait for its promoter/backstop instead of duplicating or re-filing it; only the existing server-derived dispatch/force paths may grant their narrower exceptions."
        },

          "summary": "Watchdog signal (routine-failure): the background routine \"reconcile-silent-halts\" recorded an error and may be silently wedged (the underlying cause varies by routine — check metadata.last_error_source).\n\nLast error: [db-call-deadline] \"boundedPgReadTxn:acquire(admin)\" did not resolve within 8000ms (waited 8000ms).           "detail": "duplicate screening is pending for this WORK-ITEM CLAIM. It does not block opening an agent or fleet process; launch and queue admission are separate capabilities. A launched agent still needs claimable or validly pre-assigned work. This row carries DUPLICATE CANDIDATES found when it was filed, at least one stronger than a soft semantic match, so only the promoter's model review can admit it (as distinct) or merge it into its twin. The promoter runs on a 30-minute tick but reviews only a few rows per tick, candidate rows first, so this wait has NO fixed bound; the independent fail-open runner deliberately never admits a row with unresolved strong candidates. It still CLEARS ON ITS OWN, and it is not a permanent refusal — but do not wait on it: check whether a named candidate is the same problem and work that row instead, or use a create-time admission route for genuinely distinct work.",
          "failOpenExclusion": "duplicate-candidates",
          "remedy": "Choose the work route before creating a replacement. Existing-plan fleet work should use plans:start then fleet:launch-on-plan; its promoted items use the plan-item bypass. Fixed ad-hoc work should be created atomically with assign_to naming the intended owner, then launched with capability:launch-agent. Any one of these makes a NEW item born admitted: the item implements a plan item; severity:'critical'; a 'security' topic; assign_to names the intended owner. These are create-time admission bypasses, not blanket authority: assign_to is still checked against the target's live fleet scope, dependencies, locks, security, credentials and resource admission; a scope mismatch files the item unassigned and explains why. Launching does not change an ALREADY-pending item. Wait for its promoter/backstop instead of duplicating or re-filing it; only the existing server-derived dispatch/force paths may grant their narrower exceptions."

          "detail": "duplicate screening is pending for this WORK-ITEM CLAIM. It does not block opening an agent or fleet process; launch and queue admission are separate capabilities. A launched agent still needs claimable or validly pre-assigned work. This row carries DUPLICATE CANDIDATES found when it was filed, at least one stronger than a soft semantic match, so only the promoter's model review can admit it (as distinct) or merge it into its twin. The promoter runs on a 30-minute tick but reviews only a few rows per tick, candidate rows first, so this wait has NO fixed bound; the independent fail-open runner deliberately never admits a row with unresolved strong candidates. It still CLEARS ON ITS OWN, and it is not a permanent refusal — but do not wait on it: check whether a named candidate is the same problem and work that row instead, or use a create-time admission route for genuinely distinct work.",
          "failOpenExclusion": "duplicate-candidates",
          "remedy": "Choose the work route before creating a replacement. Existing-plan fleet work should use plans:start then fleet:launch-on-plan; its promoted items use the plan-item bypass. Fixed ad-hoc work should be created atomically with assign_to naming the intended owner, then launched with capability:launch-agent. Any one of these makes a NEW item born admitted: the item implements a plan item; severity:'critical'; a 'security' topic; assign_to names the intended owner. These are create-time admission bypasses, not blanket authority: assign_to is still checked against the target's live fleet scope, dependencies, locks, security, credentials and resource admission; a scope mismatch files the item unassigned and explains why. Launching does not change an ALREADY-pending item. Wait for its promoter/backstop instead of duplicating or re-filing it; only the existing server-derived dispatch/force paths may grant their narrower exceptions."
        },
